\Pi_{\mathsf{mult}}
protocol on
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
.

Lemma 3.9

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{BitInj}}
requires an amortized communication cost of
12\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase and requires
2
rounds and an amortized communication of
7\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{BitInj}}
is essentially an execution of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Lemma
3.8
) followed by one invocation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult}}
(Lemma
3.5
) and the costs follow.
∎

Dot Product

Given the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of vectors
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
and
\vec{\mathbf{y}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
11
) allows servers to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
robustly.
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of a vector
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
of size
\mathsf{n}
, means that each element
\mathsf{x}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
of
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
, for
i\in[\mathsf{n}]
, is
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shared.
We borrow ideas from BLAZE for obtaining an online communication cost
independent
of
\mathsf{n}
and use
\mathsf{jmp}
primitive to ensure either success or
\mathsf{TTP}
selection. Analogous to our multiplication protocol, our dot product offloads one call to a robust dot product protocol to the preprocessing. By extending techniques of
[
9
,
10
]
, we give an instantiation for the dot product protocol used in our preprocessing whose (amortized) communication cost is constant, thereby making our preprocessing cost also
independent
of
\mathsf{n}
.

To begin with,
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
can be viewed as
\mathsf{n}
parallel multiplication instances of the form
\mathsf{z}_{i}=\mathsf{x}_{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}
for
i\in[n]
, followed by adding up the results. Let
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}_{i}}^{\star}
. Then,

\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}})\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}+{\chi}

(3)

where
\chi=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}}-\psi_{i})
.

Apart from the aforementioned modification, the online phase for dot product proceeds similar to that of multiplication protocol.
P_{0},P_{1}
locally compute
\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}
as per Eq.
3
and
\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
.
P_{1}
obtains
\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
in a similar fashion.
P_{1},P_{2}
reconstruct
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}+\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
and compute
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\psi
. Here, the value
\psi
has to be correctly generated in the preprocessing phase satisfying Eq.
3
. Finally,
P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
.

We now provide the details for preprocessing phase that enable servers to obtain the required values (
\chi,\psi
) with the invocation of a dot product protocol in a black-box way. Towards this, let
\vec{\mathbf{d}}=[\mathsf{d}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{n}}]
and
\vec{\mathbf{e}}=[\mathsf{e}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{n}}]
, where
\mathsf{d}_{i}=\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}=\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}
for
i\in[\mathsf{n}]
, as in the case of multiplication. Then for
\mathsf{f}=\vec{\mathbf{d}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{e}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}

\displaystyle=\vec{\mathbf{d}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{e}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\mathsf{d}_{i}\mathsf{e}_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}})(\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\psi_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\chi_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\psi_{i})+\chi

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\psi_{i})+\left[\chi\right]_{1}+\left[\chi\right]_{2}=\mathsf{f}_{2}+\mathsf{f}_{1}+\mathsf{f}_{0}.

where
\mathsf{f}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\psi_{i}),\mathsf{f}_{1}=\left[\chi\right]_{1}
and
\mathsf{f}_{0}=\left[\chi\right]_{2}
.

Using the above relation, the preprocessing phase proceeds as follows:
P_{0},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
sample a random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while
P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
. Servers locally prepare
\langle\vec{\mathbf{d}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{e}}\rangle
similar to that of multiplication protocol. Servers then execute a robust 3PC dot product protocol, denoted by
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
(the task is abstracted away in the functionality Fig.
12
), that takes
\langle\vec{\mathbf{d}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{e}}\rangle
as input and compute
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle
with
\mathsf{f}=\vec{\mathbf{d}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{e}}
. Given
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle
, the
\psi
and
\left[\chi\right]
values are extracted as follows (ref. Eq.
4
):

\psi=\mathsf{f}_{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}},~~\left[\chi\right]_{1}=\mathsf{f}_{1},~~\left[\chi\right]_{2}=\mathsf{f}_{0},

(4)

It is easy to see from the semantics of
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing that both
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain
\mathsf{f}_{2}
and hence
\psi
. Similarly, both
P_{0},P_{1}
obtain
\mathsf{f}_{1}
and hence
\left[\chi\right]_{1}
, while
P_{0},P_{2}
obtain
\left[\chi\right]_{2}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

–

P_{0},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, together sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while
P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

Servers locally compute
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\vec{\mathbf{d}},\vec{\mathbf{e}}
with
\mathsf{d}_{i}=\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}=\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}
for
i\in[\mathsf{n}]
as follows:

\displaystyle({\langle\mathsf{d}_{i}\rangle}_{0}\text{=}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{2},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{1}),{\langle\mathsf{d}_{i}\rangle}_{1}\text{=}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{1},\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}),{\langle\mathsf{d}_{i}\rangle}_{2}\text{=}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{2}))

\displaystyle({\langle\mathsf{e}_{i}\rangle}_{0}\text{=}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{2},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{1}),{\langle\mathsf{e}_{i}\rangle}_{1}\text{=}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{1},\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}),{\langle\mathsf{e}_{i}\rangle}_{2}\text{=}(\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{2}))

–

Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}(\mathcal{P},\langle\vec{\mathbf{d}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{e}}\rangle)
to generate
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle=\langle\vec{\mathbf{d}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{e}}\rangle
.

–

P_{0},P_{1}
locally set
\left[\chi\right]_{1}=\mathsf{f}_{1}
, while
P_{0},P_{2}
locally set
\left[\chi\right]_{2}=\mathsf{f}_{0}
.
P_{1},P_{2}
locally compute
\psi=\mathsf{f}_{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}
.

\justify

Online:

–

P_{0},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, compute
\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{j}=-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}((\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{j}+(\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{j})+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}+\left[\chi\right]_{j}
.

–

P_{0},P_{1}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{0},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}
locally compute
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}+\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
and set
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})+\psi
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
.

Figure 11
:
3PC: Dot Product Protocol (
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
)

\justify

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{DotPPre}}
interacts with the servers in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{DotPPre}}
receives
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of vectors
\vec{\mathbf{d}}=(\mathsf{d}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{d}_{\mathsf{n}}),\vec{\mathbf{e}}=(\mathsf{e}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{n}})
from the servers.
Let
\mathsf{v}_{j,s}
for
j\in[\mathsf{n}],s\in\{0,1,2\}
denote the share of
\mathsf{v}_{j}
such that
\mathsf{v}_{j}=\mathsf{v}_{j,0}+\mathsf{v}_{j,1}+\mathsf{v}_{j,2}
.
Server
P_{s}
, for
s\in\{0,1,2\}
, holds
\langle\mathsf{d}_{j}\rangle_{s}=(\mathsf{d}_{j,s},\mathsf{d}_{j,(s+1)\%3})
and
\langle\mathsf{e}_{j}\rangle_{s}=(\mathsf{e}_{j,s},\mathsf{e}_{j,(s+1)\%3})
where
j\in[\mathsf{n}]
. Let
P_{i}
denotes the server corrupted by
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{MulPre}}
receives
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{i}=(\mathsf{f}_{i},\mathsf{f}_{(i+1)\%3})
from
\mathcal{S}
where
\mathsf{f}=\vec{\mathbf{d}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{e}}
.

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{DotPPre}}
proceeds as follows:

–

Reconstructs
\mathsf{d}_{j},\mathsf{e}_{j}
, for
j\in[\mathsf{n}]
, using the shares received from honest servers and compute
\mathsf{f}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathsf{d}_{j}\mathsf{e}_{j}
.

–

Compute
\mathsf{f}_{(i+2)\%3}=\mathsf{f}-\mathsf{f}_{i}-\mathsf{f}_{(i+1)\%3}
and set the output shares as
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{0}=(\mathsf{f}_{0},\mathsf{f}_{1}),\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{1}=(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2}),\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{2}=(\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{0})
.

–

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{s})
to server
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
.

Figure 12
:
3PC: Ideal functionality for
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
protocol

The ideal world functionality for realizing
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
is presented in Fig.
12
.
A trivial way to instantiate
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
is to treat a dot product operation as
\mathsf{n}
multiplications. However, this results in a communication cost that is linearly dependent on the feature size.
Instead, we instantiate
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
by a semi-honest dot product protocol followed by a verification phase to check the correctness. For the verification phase, we extend the techniques of
[
9
,
10
]
to provide support for verification of dot product tuples. Setting the verification phase parameters appropriately gives a
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
whose (amortized) communication cost is independent of the feature size. Details appear in §
B
.

Lemma 3.10

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
11
) requires an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase and requires
1
round and an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

During the preprocessing phase, servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
. This requires communicating
3\ell
bits for a single semi-honest dot product protocol and
\mathcal{O}(\frac{\sqrt{\mathsf{n}}}{\sqrt{m}})
extended ring elements for its verification. By appropriately setting the values of
\mathsf{n},m
, the cost of communicating
\mathcal{O}(\frac{\sqrt{\mathsf{n}}}{\sqrt{m}})
elements can be amortized away, thereby resulting in an amortized communication cost of
3\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase.
The online phase follows similarly to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult}}
, the only difference being that servers combine their shares corresponding to all the
\mathsf{n}
multiplications into one and then exchange. This requires one round and an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits.
∎

Truncation

Working over fixed-point values, repeated multiplications using FPA arithmetic can lead to an overflow resulting in loss of significant bits of information. This put forth the need for truncation
[
45
,
43
,
15
,
12
,
48
]
that re-adjusts the shares after multiplication so that FPA semantics are maintained. As shown in SecureML
[
45
]
, the method of truncation would result in loss of information on the least significant bits and affect the accuracy by a very minimal amount.

For truncation, servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
13
) to generate
(\left[\mathsf{r}\right],\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket)
-pair, where
\mathsf{r}
is a random ring element, and
\mathsf{r}^{d}
is the truncated value of
\mathsf{r}
, i.e the value
\mathsf{r}
right-shifted by
d
bit positions. Recall that
d
denotes the number of bits allocated for the fractional part in the FPA representation. Given
(\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d})
, the truncated value of
\mathsf{v}
, denoted as
{\mathsf{v}}^{d}
, is computed as
{\mathsf{v}}^{d}={(\mathsf{v}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}+\mathsf{r}^{d}
. The correctness and accuracy of this method was shown in ABY3
[
43
]
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
is inspired from
[
43
,
16
]
and proceeds as follows to generate
(\left[\mathsf{r}\right],\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket)
. Analogous to the approach of ABY3
[
43
]
, servers generate a boolean sharing of an
\ell
-bit value
\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{r}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}
, non-interactively. Each server truncates its share of
\mathsf{r}
locally to obtain a boolean sharing of
\mathsf{r}^{d}
by removing the lower
d
bits. To obtain the arithmetic shares of
(\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d})
from their boolean sharing, we do not, however, rely on the approach of ABY3 as it requires more rounds. Instead, we implicitly perform a
boolean to arithmetic conversion
, as was proposed in Trident
[
16
]
, to obtain the arithmetic shares of
(\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d})
. This entails performing two dot product operations and constitutes the cost for
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
.

\justify

–

To generate each bit
\mathsf{r}[i]
of
\mathsf{r}
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
,
P_{0},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
sample random
\mathsf{r}_{j}[i]\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and define
\mathsf{r}[i]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]
.

–

Servers generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
(\mathsf{r}_{j}[i])^{\sf R}
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\},j\in\{1,2\}
non-interactively following Table
2
.

–

Define
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
and
\vec{\mathbf{y}}
such that
\mathsf{x}=2^{i-d+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{y}_{i}=(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
, respectively, for
i\in\{d,\ldots,\ell-1\}
.
Define
\vec{\mathbf{p}}
and
\vec{\mathbf{q}}
such that
\mathsf{p}_{i}=2^{i+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{q}_{i}=(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
, respectively, for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
. Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
to compute
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{A}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
and
\mathsf{B}=\vec{\mathbf{p}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{q}}
.

–

Servers locally compute
\llbracket{\mathsf{r}^{d}}\rrbracket=\sum_{i=d}^{\ell-1}2^{i-d}(\llbracket({\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]})^{\sf R}\rrbracket+\llbracket(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\rrbracket)-\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket
, and
\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\llbracket({\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]})^{\sf R}\rrbracket+\llbracket({\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]})^{\sf R}\rrbracket)-\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket
.

–

P_{0}
locally computes
\beta_{\mathsf{r}}=\mathsf{r}+\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}
.
P_{0},P_{1}
set
\left[\mathsf{r}\right]_{1}=-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}\right]_{1}
and
P_{0},P_{2}
set
\left[\mathsf{r}\right]_{2}=\beta_{\mathsf{r}}-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}\right]_{2}
.

Figure 13
:
3PC: Generating Random Truncated Pair
(\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d})

We now give details for generating
(\left[\mathsf{r}\right],\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket)
. For this, servers proceed as follows:
P_{0},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
sample random
\mathsf{r}_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Recall that the bit at
i\text{th}
position in
\mathsf{r}
is denoted as
\mathsf{r}[i]
. Define
\mathsf{r}[i]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
. For
\mathsf{r}
defined as above, we have
\mathsf{r}^{d}[i]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[i+d]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i+d]
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-d-1\}
. Further,

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{r}[i]=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}-2(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\left(2^{i+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\right)\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}

(5)

Similarly, for
\mathsf{r}^{d}
we have the following,

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=d}^{\ell-1}2^{i-d}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)-\sum_{i=d}^{\ell-1}\left(2^{i-d+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\right)\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}

(6)

The servers non-interactively generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares (arithmetic shares) for each bit of
\mathsf{r}_{1}
and
\mathsf{r}_{2}
as in Table
2
.
Given their
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares, the servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
twice to compute
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of
\mathsf{A}=\sum_{i=d}^{\ell-1}(2^{i-d+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R})\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
, and
\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}(2^{i+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R})\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
. Using these values, the servers can locally compute the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares for (
\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d}
) pair following Equation
3.3
and
6
, respectively. Note that servers need
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\mathsf{r}
and not
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares. The
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares can be computed from the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares locally as follows. Let
(\alpha_{\mathsf{r}},\beta_{\mathsf{r}},\gamma_{\mathsf{r}})
be the values corresponding to the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{r}
. Since
P_{0}
knows the entire value
\mathsf{r}
in clear, and it knows
\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}
, it can locally compute
\beta_{\mathsf{r}}
. Now, the servers set
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares as:
\left[\mathsf{r}\right]_{1}=-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}\right]_{1}
and
\left[\mathsf{r}\right]_{2}=\beta_{\mathsf{r}}-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{r}}\right]_{2}
. The protocol appears in Fig.
13
.

Lemma 3.11

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
13
) requires an amortized communication of
12\ell
bits.

Proof.

All the operations in
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
are non-interactive except for the two dot product calls required to compute
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}
. The cost thus follows from Lemma
3.10
.
∎

Dot Product with Truncation

Given the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of vectors
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
and
\vec{\mathbf{y}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpt}}
(Fig.
14
) allows servers to generate
\llbracket{\mathsf{z}}^{d}\rrbracket
, where
{\mathsf{z}}^{d}
denotes the truncated value of
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
. A naive way is to compute the dot product using
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
, followed by performing truncation using the (
\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d}
) pair. Instead, we follow the optimization of BLAZE where the online phase of
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
is modified to integrate the truncation using
(\mathsf{r},\mathsf{r}^{d})
at no additional cost.

The preprocessing phase now consists of the execution of one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
13
) and the preprocessing corresponding to
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
11
). In the online phase, servers enable
P_{1},P_{2}
to obtain
\mathsf{z}^{\star}-\mathsf{r}
instead of
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}
, where
\mathsf{z}^{\star}=\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}-\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}
. Using
\mathsf{z}^{\star}-\mathsf{r}
, both
P_{1},P_{2}
then compute
(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})
locally, truncate it to obtain
{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}
and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh}}
to generate
\llbracket{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}\rrbracket
. Finally, servers locally compute the result as
\llbracket{\mathsf{z}}^{d}\rrbracket=\llbracket{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket
.
The formal details for
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpt}}
protocol appear in Fig.
14
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

–

Servers execute the preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}(\mathcal{P},\{\llbracket\mathsf{x}_{i}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{i}\rrbracket\}_{i\in[\mathsf{n}]})
.

–

In parallel, servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}(\mathcal{P})
to generate the truncation pair
(\left[\mathsf{r}\right],\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket)
.

\justify

Online:

–

P_{0},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, compute
\left[\Psi\right]_{j}=-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}((\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{j}+(\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{j})-\left[\mathsf{r}\right]_{j}
and set
\left[{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\star}}\right]_{j}=\left[\Psi\right]_{j}+\left[\chi\right]_{j}
.

–

P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\left[{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{\star}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{2},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp\mbox{-}send}

\left[{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}
locally compute
(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\star}=\left[{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{\star}\right]_{1}+\left[{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{\star}\right]_{2}
and set
(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})={(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{\star}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})+\psi
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}
locally truncate
(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})
to obtain
{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}
and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh}}(P_{1},P_{2},{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d})
to generate
\llbracket{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}\rrbracket
.

–

Servers locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket=\llbracket{(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})}^{d}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{d}\rrbracket
.

Figure 14
:
3PC: Dot Product Protocol with Truncation

Lemma 3.12

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpt}}
(Fig.
14
) requires an amortized communication of
15\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase and requires
1
round and an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

During the preprocessing phase, servers execute the preprocessing phase of
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
, resulting in an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits (Lemma
3.10
). In parallel, servers execute one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
protocol resulting in an additional communication of
12\ell
bits (Lemma
3.11
).

The online phase follows from that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
protocol except that, now,
P_{1},P_{2}
compute additive shares of
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
, where
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
, which is achieved using two executions of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp}}
in parallel. This requires one round and an amortized communication cost of
2\ell
bits.
P_{1},P_{2}
then jointly share the truncated value of
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
with
P_{0}
, which requires one round and
\ell
bits. However, this step can be deferred till the end for multiple dot product with truncation instances, which amortizes the cost.
∎

Secure Comparison

Secure comparison allows servers to check whether
\mathsf{x}<\mathsf{y}
, given their
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares. In FPA representation, checking
\mathsf{x}<\mathsf{y}
is equivalent to checking the
\mathsf{msb}
of
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{x}-\mathsf{y}
. Towards this, servers locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket
and extract the
\mathsf{msb}
of
\mathsf{v}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext}}
. In case an arithmetic sharing is desired, servers can apply
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
10
) protocol on the outcome of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext}}
protocol.

Activation Functions

We now elaborate on two of the most prominently used activation functions: i) Rectified Linear Unit (ReLU) and (ii) Sigmoid (Sig).

(i) ReLU:

The ReLU function,
\mathsf{relu}(\mathsf{v})=\mathsf{max}(0,\mathsf{v})
, can be viewed as
\mathsf{relu}(\mathsf{v})=\overline{\mathsf{b}}\cdot\mathsf{v}
, where bit
\mathsf{b}=1
if
\mathsf{v}<0
and
0
otherwise. Here
\overline{\mathsf{b}}
denotes the complement of
\mathsf{b}
.
Given
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext}}
on
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
.
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\bf B}
-sharing of
\overline{\mathsf{b}}
is locally computed by setting
\beta_{\overline{\mathsf{b}}}=1\oplus\beta_{\mathsf{b}}
. Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{BitInj}}
protocol on
\llbracket\overline{\mathsf{b}}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
to obtain the desired result.

Lemma 3.13

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\mathsf{relu}
requires an amortized communication of
21\ell-6
bits in the preprocessing phase and requires
\log\ell+3
rounds and an amortized communication of
16\ell-6
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

One instance of
\mathsf{relu}
protocol comprises of execution of one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext}}
, followed by
\Pi_{\mathsf{BitInj}}
. The cost, therefore, follows from Lemma
3.7
, and Lemma
3.9
.
∎

(ii) Sig:

In this work, we use the MPC-friendly variant of the Sigmoid function
[
45
,
43
,
15
]
.
Note that
\mathsf{sig}(\mathsf{v})=\overline{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{b}_{2}(\mathsf{v}+1/2)+\overline{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
, where
\mathsf{b}_{1}=1
if
\mathsf{v}+1/2<0
and
\mathsf{b}_{2}=1
if
\mathsf{v}-1/2<0
. To compute
\llbracket\mathsf{sig}(\mathsf{v})\rrbracket
, servers proceed in a similar fashion as in ReLU, and hence, we skip the details.

The formal details of the MPC-friendly variant of the Sigmoid function
[
45
,
43
,
15
]
is given below:

\displaystyle\mathsf{sig}(\mathsf{v})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}0&\quad\mathsf{v}<-\frac{1}{2}\\
\mathsf{v}+\frac{1}{2}&\quad-\frac{1}{2}\leq\mathsf{v}\leq\frac{1}{2}\\
1&\quad\mathsf{v}>\frac{1}{2}\end{array}\right.

Lemma 3.14

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\mathsf{sig}
requires an amortized communication of
39\ell-9
bits in the preprocessing phase and requires
\log\ell+4
rounds and an amortized communication of
29\ell-9
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

An instance of
\mathsf{sig}
protocol involves the execution of the following protocols in order– i) two parallel instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext}}
protocol, ii) once instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult}}
protocol over boolean value, and iii) one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{BitInj}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
in parallel. The cost follows from Lemma
3.7
, Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
3.9
.
∎

Maxpool, Matrix Operations and Convolutions

The goal of maxpool is to find the maximum value in a vector
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
of
m
values. Maximum between two elements
\mathsf{x}_{i}
,
\mathsf{x}_{j}
can be computed by applying secure comparison, which returns a binary sharing of a bit
\mathsf{b}
such that
\mathsf{b}=0
if
\mathsf{x}_{i}>\mathsf{x}_{j}
, or
1
, otherwise, followed by computing
(\mathsf{b})^{\bf B}(\mathsf{x}_{j}-\mathsf{x}_{i})+\mathsf{x}_{i}
, which can be performed using bit injection (
3.3
).
To find the maximum value in vector
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
, the servers first group the values in
\vec{\mathbf{x}}
into pairs and securely compare each pair to obtain the maximum of the two. This results in a vector of size
m/2
. This process is repeated for
\operatorname{O}\left(\log m\right)
rounds to obtain the maximum value in the entire vector.

Linear matrix operations, such as addition of two matrices
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
to generate matrix
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B}
, can be computed by extending the scalar operations (addition, in this case) with respect to each element of the matrix. Matrix multiplication, on the other hand, can be expressed as a collection of dot products, where the element in the
i^{\text{th}}
row and
j^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{A}\times\mathbf{B}
, where
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
are matrices of dimension
\mathsf{p}\times\mathsf{q}
,
\mathsf{q}\times\mathsf{r}
, respectively, can be computed as a dot product of the
i^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{A}
and the
j^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathbf{B}
. Thus, computing
\mathbf{C}
of dimension
\mathsf{p}\times\mathsf{r}
requires
\mathsf{p}\mathsf{r}
dot products whose communication cost (amortized) is equal to that of computing
\mathsf{p}\mathsf{r}
multiplications in our case. This improves the cost of matrix multiplication over the naive approach which requires
\mathsf{p}\mathsf{q}\mathsf{r}
multiplications.

Convolutions form an important building block in several neural network architectures and can be represented as matrix multiplications, as explained in the example below. Consider a 2-dimensional convolution (
\mathsf{CV}
) of a
3\times 3
input matrix
\mathbf{X}
with a kernel
\mathbf{K}
of size
2\times 2
. This can be represented as a matrix multiplication as follows.

\mathsf{CV}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{x}_{1}&\mathsf{x}_{2}&\mathsf{x}_{3}\\
\mathsf{x}_{4}&\mathsf{x}_{5}&\mathsf{x}_{6}\\
\mathsf{x}_{7}&\mathsf{x}_{8}&\mathsf{x}_{9}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{k}_{1}&\mathsf{k}_{2}\\
\mathsf{k}_{3}&\mathsf{k}_{4}\end{bmatrix}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{x}_{1}&\mathsf{x}_{2}&\mathsf{x}_{4}&\mathsf{x}_{5}\\
\mathsf{x}_{2}&\mathsf{x}_{3}&\mathsf{x}_{5}&\mathsf{x}_{6}\\
\mathsf{x}_{4}&\mathsf{x}_{5}&\mathsf{x}_{7}&\mathsf{x}_{8}\\
\mathsf{x}_{5}&\mathsf{x}_{6}&\mathsf{x}_{8}&\mathsf{x}_{9}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{k}_{1}\\
\mathsf{k}_{2}\\
\mathsf{k}_{3}\\
\mathsf{k}_{4}\end{bmatrix}

Generally, convolving a
f\times f
kernel over a
w\times h
input with
p\times p
padding using
s\times s
stride having
i
input channels and
o
output channels, is equivalent to performing a matrix multiplication on matrices of dimension
(w^{\prime}\cdot h^{\prime})\times(i\cdot f\cdot f)
and
(i\cdot f\cdot f)\times(o)
where
w^{\prime}=\dfrac{w-f+2p}{s}+1
and
h^{\prime}=\dfrac{h-f+2p}{s}+1
. We refer readers to
[
56
]
(cf. “Linear and Convolutional Layer”) and
[
54
]
for more details.

4
Robust 4PC and PPML

In this section, we extend our 3PC results to the 4-party case and observe substantial efficiency gain. First, the use of broadcast is eliminated. Second, the preprocessing of multiplication becomes substantially computationally light, eliminating the multiplication protocol (used in the preprocessing) altogether. Third, we achieve a dot product protocol with communication cost independent of the size of the vector, completely eliminating the complex machinery required as in the 3PC case. At the heart of our 4PC constructions lies an efficient 4-party
\mathsf{jmp}
primitive, denoted as
\mathsf{jmp4}
, that allows two servers to send a common value to a third server robustly.

This section is organized as follows. We begin with the secret-sharing semantics for
4
servers, for which we only use an extended version of
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing. We then explain the joint message passing primitive for four servers, followed by our 4PC protocols. We conclude this section with a detailed analysis about achieving private robustness.

Secret Sharing Semantics

For a value
\mathsf{v}
, the shares for
P_{0},P_{1}
and
P_{2}
remain the same as that for 3PC case. That is,
P_{0}
holds
(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2},\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})
while
P_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
holds
(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{i},\beta_{\mathsf{v}},\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})
. The shares for the fourth server
P_{3}
is defined as
(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2},\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})
. Clearly, the secret is defined as
\mathsf{v}=\beta_{\mathsf{v}}-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}-\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
.

4.1
4PC Joint Message Passing Primitive

The
\mathsf{jmp4}
primitive enables two servers
P_{i}
,
P_{j}
to send a common value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
to a third server
P_{k}
, or identify a
\mathsf{TTP}
in case of any inconsistency. This primitive is analogous to
\mathsf{jmp}
(Fig.
2
) in spirit but is significantly optimized and free from broadcast calls. Similar to the 3PC counterpart, each server maintains a bit and
P_{i}
sends the value, and
P_{j}
the hash of it to
P_{k}
.
P_{k}
sets its inconsistency bit to
1
when the (value, hash) pair is inconsistent. This is followed by relaying the bit to all the servers, who exchange it among themselves and agree on the bit that forms majority (
1
indicates the presence of inconsistency, and
0
indicates consistency). The presence of an honest majority among
P_{i},P_{j},P_{l}
, guarantees agreement on the presence/absence of an inconsistency as conveyed by
P_{k}
.
Observe that inconsistency can only be caused either due to a corrupt sender sending an incorrect value (or hash), or a corrupt receiver falsely announcing the presence of inconsistency. Hence, the fourth server,
P_{l}
, can safely be employed as
\mathsf{TTP}
. The ideal functionality appears in Fig.
15
, and the protocol appears in Fig.
16
.

Notation 4.1
.

We say that
P_{i},P_{j}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
when they invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}(P_{i},P_{j},P_{k},\mathsf{v},P_{l})
.

We note that the end goal of
\mathsf{jmp4}
primitive relates closely to the bi-convey primitive of FLASH
[
12
]
. Bi-convey allows two servers
S_{1},S_{2}
to convey a value to a server
R
, and in case of an inconsistency, a pair of honest servers mutually identify each other, followed by exchanging their internal randomness to recover the clear inputs, computing the circuit, and sending the output to all.
Note, however, that
\mathsf{jmp4}
primitive is more efficient and differs significantly in techniques from the bi-convey primitive. Unlike in bi-convey, in case of an inconsistency,
\mathsf{jmp4}
enables servers to learn the
\mathsf{TTP}
’s identity unanimously. Moreover, bi-convey demands that honest servers, identified during an inconsistency, exchange their internal randomness (which comprises of the shared keys established during the key-setup phase) to proceed with the computation. This enforces the need for a fresh key-setup every time inconsistency is detected.
On the efficiency front,
\mathsf{jmp4}
simply halves the communication cost of bi-convey, giving a
2\times
improvement.

\justify

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
interacts with the servers in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.

Step 1:

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{jmp}}
receives
(\mathsf{Input},\mathsf{v}_{s})
from senders
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
,
(\mathsf{Input},\bot)
from receiver
P_{k}
and fourth server
P_{l}
, while it receives
(\mathsf{Select},\mathsf{ttp})
from
\mathcal{S}
. Here
\mathsf{ttp}
is a boolean value, with a
1
indicating that
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{l}
should be established.

Step 2:

If
\mathsf{v}_{i}=\mathsf{v}_{j}
and
\mathsf{ttp}=0
, or if
\mathcal{S}
has corrupted
P_{l}
, set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\mathsf{msg}_{l}=\bot,\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{v}_{i}
and go to
Step 4
.

Step 3:

Else : Set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{msg}_{l}=P_{l}
.

Step 4:

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\mathsf{msg}_{s})
to
P_{s}
for
s\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.

Figure 15
:
4PC: Ideal functionality for
\mathsf{jmp4}
primitive

P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
initializes an inconsistency bit
\mathsf{b}_{s}=0
. If
P_{s}
remains silent instead of sending
\mathsf{b}_{s}
in any of the following rounds, the recipient sets
\mathsf{b}_{s}
to
1
.

\justify

Send Phase:

P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
.

Verify Phase:

P_{j}
sends
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
to
P_{k}
.

–

P_{k}
sets
\mathsf{b}_{k}=1
if the received values are inconsistent or if the value is not received.

–

P_{k}
sends
\mathsf{b}_{k}
to all servers.
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,l\}
sets
\mathsf{b}_{s}=\mathsf{b}_{k}
.

–

P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,l\}
mutually exchange their bits.
P_{s}
resets
\mathsf{b}_{s}=\mathsf{b}^{\prime}
where
\mathsf{b}^{\prime}
denotes the bit which appears in majority among
\mathsf{b}_{i},\mathsf{b}_{j},\mathsf{b}_{l}
.

–

All servers set
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{l}
if
\mathsf{b}^{\prime}=1
, terminate otherwise.

Figure 16
:
4PC: Joint Message Passing Primitive

Lemma 4.2

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
(Fig.
16
) requires
1
round and an amortized communication of
\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

Server
P_{i}
sends the value
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
while
P_{j}
sends hash of the same to
P_{k}
. This accounts for one round of communication. Values sent by
P_{j}
for several instances can be concatenated and hashed to obtain a single value. Hence the cost of sending the hash gets amortized over multiple instances. Similarly, the two round exchange of inconsistency bits to establish a
\mathsf{TTP}
can be combined for multiple instances, thereby amortizing this cost. Thus, the amortized cost of this protocol is
\ell
bits.
∎

4.2
4PC Protocols

In this section, we revisit the protocols from 3PC (§
3
) and suggest optimizations leveraging the presence of an additional honest party in the system. We provide security proofs in §
C.2
.

Sharing Protocol

To enable
P_{i}
to share a value
\mathsf{v}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{sh4}}
(Fig.
17
) proceeds similar to that of 3PC case with the addition that
P_{3}
also samples the values
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2},\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
using the shared randomness with the respective servers. On a high level,
P_{i}
computes
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
and sends
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
(or
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
) to another server and they together
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}
this information to the intended servers. The formal protocol for sharing a value
\mathsf{v}
by
P_{i}
is given in Fig.
17

Preprocessing:

–

If
P_{i}=P_{0}
:
P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, together sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while
\mathcal{P}
sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

If
P_{i}=P_{1}
:
P_{0},P_{3},P_{1}
together sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while
\mathcal{P}
sample a random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Also,
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

If
P_{i}=P_{2}
: Analogous to the case when
P_{i}=P_{1}
.

–

If
P_{i}=P_{3}
:
P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
together sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

\justify

Online:

–

If
P_{i}=P_{0}
:
P_{0}
computes
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{1}
.
P_{0},P_{1}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{2}
.

–

If
P_{i}=P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
:
P_{j}
computes
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
, sends
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{3-j}
.

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
.

–

If
P_{i}=P_{3}
:
P_{3}
sends
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
.

P_{3},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

Figure 17
:
4PC: Generating
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
-shares by server
P_{i}

Lemma 4.3

(Communication)
.

In the online phase,
\Pi_{\mathsf{sh4}}
(Fig.
17
) requires
2
rounds and an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits when
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
share a value, whereas it requires an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits when
P_{3}
shares a value.

Proof.

The proof for
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sharing a value follows from
3.3
. For the case when
P_{3}
wants to share a value
\mathsf{v}
, it first sends
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
which requires one round and
\ell
bits of communication. This is followed by
2
parallel calls to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
which together require one round and an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits. ∎

Joint Sharing Protocol

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
enables a pair of (unordered) servers
(P_{i},P_{j})
to jointly generate a
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
known to both of them. In case of an inconsistency, the server outside the computation serves as a
\mathsf{TTP}
. The protocol is described in Fig.
18
.

When
P_{3},P_{0}
want to jointly share a value
\mathsf{v}
which is available in the preprocessing phase, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
can be performed with a single element of communication (as opposed to
2
elements in Fig.
18
).

P_{0},P_{3}
can jointly share
\mathsf{v}
as follows.
P_{0},P_{3},P_{1}
sample a random
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and set
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}=\mathsf{r}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
set
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}=-(\mathsf{r}+\mathsf{v})
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
. This is followed by servers locally setting
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}=\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=0
.

We further observe that servers can generate a
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{v}
non-interactively when
\mathsf{v}
is available with
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
. For this, servers set
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}=\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}=0
and
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}
. We abuse notation and use
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}(P_{0},P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{v})
to denote this sharing.

Lemma 4.4

(Communication)
.

In the online phase,
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
(Fig.
18
) requires
1
round and an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits when
(P_{3},P_{s})
for
s\in\{0,1,2\}
share a value, and requires an amortized communication of
\ell
bits, otherwise.

Proof.

When
(P_{3},P_{s})
for
s\in\{0,1,2\}
want to share a value
\mathsf{v}
, there are two parallel calls to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
which requires an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits and one round. In the other cases,
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
is invoked only once, resulting in an amortized communication of
\ell
bits.
∎

Preprocessing:

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{1},P_{2})
:
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Servers locally set
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}=\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}=0
.

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{s},P_{0})
, for
s\in\{1,2\}
: Servers execute the preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{sh4}}(P_{s},\mathsf{v})
. Servers locally set
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}=0
.

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{s},P_{3})
, for
s\in\{0,1,2\}
: Servers execute the preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{sh4}}(P_{s},\mathsf{v})
.

\justify

Online:

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{1},P_{2})
:
P_{1},P_{2}
set
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
.

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{s},P_{0})
, for
s\in\{1,2,3\}
:
P_{s},P_{0}
compute
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{k}
, where
(k\in\{1,2\})\wedge(k\neq s)
.

–

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{s},P_{3})
, for
s\in\{1,2\}
:

P_{3},P_{s}
compute
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
.
P_{s},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{k}
, where
(k\in\{1,2\})\wedge(k\neq s)
. In parallel,
P_{s},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
.

Figure 18
:
4PC:
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
jointly by
P_{i},P_{j}

\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing Protocol

In some protocols,
P_{3}
is required to generate
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of a value
\mathsf{v}
in the preprocessing phase, where
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\mathsf{v}
is same as that defined in 3PC (where
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}_{0}+\mathsf{v}_{1}+\mathsf{v}_{2}
, and
P_{0}
possesses
(\mathsf{v}_{0},\mathsf{v}_{1})
,
P_{1}
possesses
(\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2})
, and
P_{2}
possess
(\mathsf{v}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{0})
) with the addition that
P_{3}
now possesses
(\mathsf{v}_{0},\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2})
. We call the resultant protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}
and it appears in Fig.
19
.

Preprocessing

:

–

Servers
P_{0},P_{3},P_{1}
sample a random
\mathsf{v}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while servers
P_{0},P_{3},P_{2}
sample a random
\mathsf{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

P_{3}
computes
\mathsf{v}_{2}=\mathsf{v}-\mathsf{v}_{0}-\mathsf{v}_{1}
and sends
\mathsf{v}_{2}
to
P_{2}
.
P_{3},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{v}_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

Figure 19
:
4PC:
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of value
\mathsf{v}
by
P_{3}

Note that servers can locally convert
\langle\mathsf{v}\rangle
to
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
by setting their shares as shown in Table
5
.

P_{0}

P_{1}

P_{2}

P_{3}

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket

(-\mathsf{v}_{1},-\mathsf{v}_{0},0)

(-\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2},-\mathsf{v}_{2})

(-\mathsf{v}_{0},\mathsf{v}_{2},-\mathsf{v}_{2})

(-\mathsf{v}_{0},-\mathsf{v}_{1},-\mathsf{v}_{2})

Table 5
:
Local conversion of shares from
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing to
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing for a value
\mathsf{v}
. Here,
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}=-\mathsf{v}_{1},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}=-\mathsf{v}_{0},\beta_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}_{2},\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}=-\mathsf{v}_{2}
.

Lemma 4.5

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}
(Fig.
19
) requires
2
rounds and an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits.

Proof.

Communicating
\mathsf{v}_{2}
to
P_{2}
requires
\ell
bits and
1
round. This is followed by one invocation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
which requires
\ell
bits and
1
round. Thus, the amortized communication cost is
2\ell
bits and two rounds.
∎

Multiplication Protocol

Given the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{x}
and
\mathsf{y}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}
(Fig.
20
) allows servers to compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket
with
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}
. When compared with the state-of-the-art 4PC GOD protocol of FLASH
[
12
]
, our solution improves communication in both, the preprocessing and online phase, from
6
to
3
ring elements. Moreover, our communication cost matches with the state-of-the-art 4PC protocol of Trident
[
16
]
that only provides security with fairness.

Recall that the goal of preprocessing in 3PC multiplication was to enable
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain
\psi
, and
P_{0},P_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
obtain
\left[\chi\right]_{i}
where
\chi=\gamma_{\mathsf{x}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}}\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}+\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}-\psi
. Here
\psi
is a random value known to both
P_{1},P_{2}
. With the help of
P_{3}
, we let the servers obtain the respective preprocessing data as follows:
P_{0},P_{3},P_{1}
together samples random
\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
locally compute
\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}
, set
\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{2}=\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}-\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{1}
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
.
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
locally sample
\psi,\mathsf{r}
and generate
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\psi
by setting
\left[\psi\right]_{1}=r
and
\left[\psi\right]_{2}=\psi-\mathsf{r}
.
Then
P_{j},P_{3}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
compute
\left[\chi\right]_{j}=\gamma_{\mathsf{x}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}\right]_{j}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}\right]_{j}+\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{j}-\left[\psi\right]_{j}
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{j}
to
P_{0}
. The online phase is similar to that of 3PC, apart from
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
being used instead of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp}}
for communication. Since
P_{3}
is not involved in the online computation phase, we can safely assume
P_{3}
to serve as the
\mathsf{TTP}
for the
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
executions in the online phase.

\justify

Preprocessing:

–

P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, and
P_{0},P_{1},P_{3}
sample random
\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{z}},\psi,\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and set
\left[\psi\right]_{1}=\mathsf{r},\allowbreak\left[\psi\right]_{2}=\psi-\mathsf{r}
.

–

P_{0},P_{3}
set
\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{2}=\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}-\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{1}
, where
\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}
.
P_{0},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
.

–

P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, set
\left[\chi\right]_{j}=\gamma_{\mathsf{x}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}\right]_{j}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}\right]_{j}+\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{j}-\left[\psi\right]_{j}
.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{1}
to
P_{0}
, while
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{2}
to
P_{0}
.

\justify

Online:

–

P_{0},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, compute
\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{j}=-(\beta_{\mathsf{x}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{x}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}}\right]_{j}-(\beta_{\mathsf{y}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}}\right]_{j}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}+\left[\chi\right]_{j}
.

–

P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
, and
P_{2},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, computes
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}+\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
and sets
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}+\beta_{\mathsf{x}}\beta_{\mathsf{y}}+\psi
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
.

Figure 20
:
4PC: Multiplication Protocol (
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{x}\cdot\mathsf{y}
)

Lemma 4.6

(Communication)
.

\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}
(Fig.
20
) requires an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase, and
1
round with an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

In the preprocessing phase, the servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\mathsf{x}\mathsf{y}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
resulting in amortized communication of
\ell
bits. This is followed by
2
parallel invocations of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{1},\left[\chi\right]_{2}
to
P_{0}
which require an amortized communication of
2\ell
bits. Thus, the amortized communication cost in preprocessing is
3\ell
bits. In the online phase, there are
2
parallel invocations of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1},\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2},P_{1}
, respectively, which requires amortized communication of
2\ell
bits and one round. This is followed by another call to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
which requires one more round and amortized communication of
\ell
bits. However,
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}
of
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
can be delayed till the end of the protocol, and will require only one round for multiple multiplication gates and hence, can be amortized. Thus, the total number of rounds required for multiplication in the online phase is one with an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits.
∎

Reconstruction Protocol

Given
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{rec4}}
(Fig.
21
) enables servers to robustly reconstruct the value
\mathsf{v}
among the servers. Note that every server lacks one share for reconstruction and the same is available with three other servers. Hence, they communicate the missing share among themselves, and the majority value is accepted. As an optimization, two among the three servers can send the missing share while the third one can send a hash of the same for verification. Notice that, as opposed to the 3PC case, this protocol does not require commitments.
The formal protocol for reconstruction is given in Fig.
21
.

\justify

Online

–

P_{0}
receives
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
from
P_{1},P_{2}
and
\mathsf{H}(\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})
from
P_{3}
.

–

P_{1}
receives
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
from
P_{2},P_{3}
and
\mathsf{H}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2})
from
P_{0}
.

–

P_{2}
receives
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}
from
P_{0},P_{3}
and
\mathsf{H}(\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1})
from
P_{1}
.

–

P_{3}
receives
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
from
P_{0},P_{1}
and
\mathsf{H}(\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})
from
P_{2}
.

–

P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
selects the missing share forming the majority among the values received and reconstructs the output.

Figure 21
:
4PC: Reconstruction of
\mathsf{v}
among the servers

Lemma 4.7

(Communication)
.

\Pi_{\mathsf{rec4}}
(Fig.
21
) requires an amortized communication of
8\ell
bits and
1
round in the online phase.

Proof.

Each
P_{s}
for
s\in\{0,1,2,3\}
receives the missing share in clear from two other servers, while the hash of it from the third. As before, the missing share sent by the third server can be concatenated over multiple instances and hashed to obtain a single value. Thus, the amortized communication cost is
2\ell
bits per server, resulting in a total cost of
8\ell
bits.
∎

4.3
Building Blocks for PPML using 4PC

This section provides details on robust realizations of the PPML building blocks in 4-server setting (for the same blocks as in §
3.3
). We provide the security proofs in §
C.2
.

Input Sharing and Output Reconstruction in SOC Setting

We extend input sharing and reconstruction in the SOC setting as follows. To generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares for its input
\mathsf{v}
,
\mathsf{U}
receives each of the shares
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1},\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}
, and
\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
from three out of the four servers as well as a random value
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
sampled together by
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
and accepts the values that form the majority.
\mathsf{U}
locally computes
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{v}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}+\mathsf{r}
and sends
\mathsf{u}
to all the servers. Servers then execute a two round byzantine agreement (BA)
[
49
]
to agree on
\mathsf{u}
(or
\bot
).
At a high-level, the BA protocol proceeds as follows. Let us denote the value received by
P_{i}
from
\mathsf{U}
as
\mathsf{u}_{i}
. To agree on
\mathsf{u}
received from
\mathsf{U}
, the servers first arrive on an agreement regarding each
\mathsf{u}_{i}
received by
P_{i}
. This is followed by selecting the majority value among
\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{u}_{3},\mathsf{u}_{4}
. For servers to agree on
\mathsf{u}_{i}
,
P_{i}
first sends
\mathsf{u}_{i}
to all servers. This is followed by
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}\backslash P_{i}
exchanging
\mathsf{u}_{i}
among themselves. Thus, each
P_{j}\in\mathcal{P}\backslash P_{i}
receives three versions of
\mathsf{u}_{i}
and sets the majority value among the three values received as
\mathsf{u}_{i}
. Since there can be at most one corruption among the servers, the majority rule ensures that all honest servers are on the same page. Once each of the values are agreed on, every server takes the majority among
\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{u}_{3},\mathsf{u}_{4}
as the value sent by
\mathsf{U}
. If no value appears in majority, a default value is chosen.
We refer the readers to
[
49
]
for the formal details of the agreement protocol.
On successful completion of BA,
P_{0}
computes
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
from
\mathsf{u}
while
P_{1},P_{2}
compute
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
from
\mathsf{u}
locally. For the reconstruction of a value
\mathsf{v}
, servers send their
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{v}
to
\mathsf{U}
, who selects the majority value for each share and reconstructs the output. At any point, if a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified, the servers proceed as follows. All servers send their
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of the input to the
\mathsf{TTP}
.
\mathsf{TTP}
picks the majority value for each share and computes the function output. It then sends this output to
\mathsf{U}
.
\mathsf{U}
also receives the identity of the
\mathsf{TTP}
from all servers and accepts the output received from the
\mathsf{TTP}
forming majority.

Bit Extraction Protocol

This protocol enables the servers to compute a boolean sharing of the most significant bit (MSB) of a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
given the arithmetic sharing
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
. To compute the MSB, we use the optimized Parallel Prefix Adder (PPA) circuit from ABY3
[
43
]
, which takes as input two boolean values and outputs the MSB of the sum of the inputs.
The circuit requires
2(\ell-1)
AND gates and has a multiplicative depth of
\log\ell
. The protocol for bit extraction (
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext4}}
) involves computing the boolean PPA circuit using the protocols described in §
4
. The two inputs to this boolean circuit are generated as follows. The value
\mathsf{v}
whose MSB needs to be extracted can be represented as the sum of two values as
\mathsf{v}=\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+(-\alpha_{\mathsf{v}})
where the first input to the circuit will be
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
and the second input will be
-\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
. Since
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
is held by
P_{1},P_{2}
, servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
to generate
\llbracket\beta_{\mathsf{v}}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
. Similarly,
P_{0},P_{3}
possess
\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
, and servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
to generate
\llbracket-\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
. Servers in
\mathcal{P}
use the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket^{\bf B}
-shares of these two inputs (
\beta_{\mathsf{v}},-\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
) to compute the optimized PPA circuit which outputs the
\llbracket\mathsf{msb}(\mathsf{v})\rrbracket^{\bf B}
.

Lemma 4.8

(Communication)
.

The protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitext4}}
requires an amortized communication of
7\ell-6
bits in the preprocessing phase, and
\log\ell
rounds with amortized communication of
7\ell-6
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

Generation of boolean sharing of
\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}
requires
\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase (since
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
with
P_{0},P_{3}
can be achieved with
\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing phase), and generation of boolean sharing of
\beta_{\mathsf{v}}
requires
\ell
bits and one round (which can be deferred towards the end of the protocol thereby requiring one round for several instances) in the online phase. Further, the boolean PPA circuit to be computed requires
2(\ell-1)
AND gates. Since each AND gate requires
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}
to be executed, it requires an amortized communication of
6\ell-6
bits in both the preprocessing phase and the online phase. Thus, the overall communication is
7\ell-6
bits, in both, the preprocessing and online phase. The circuit has a multiplicative depth of
\log\ell
which results in
\log\ell
rounds in the online phase.
∎

Bit2A Protocol

This protocol enables servers to compute the arithmetic sharing of a bit
\mathsf{b}
given its boolean sharing. Let
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
denote the value of
\mathsf{b}
in the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. We observe that
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
can be written as follows.
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}=(\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\beta_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}=\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-2\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
.
Thus, to obtain an arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
, the servers can compute an arithmetic sharing of
\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
,
\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
and
\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
. This can be done as follows.
P_{0},P_{3}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
on
\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
in the preprocessing phase to generate
\llbracket\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
. Similarly,
P_{1},P_{2}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}
on
\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
in the online phase to generate
\llbracket\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
. This is followed by
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}
on
\llbracket\beta_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rrbracket,\llbracket\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
, followed by local computation to obtain
\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

Preprocessing

:

–

Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}(P_{3},\mathsf{e}^{\sf R})
(Fig.
19
) where
\mathsf{e}=\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}
. Let the shares be
\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle_{0}=(\mathsf{e}_{0},\mathsf{e}_{1}),\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle_{1}=(\mathsf{e}_{1},\mathsf{e}_{2}),\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle_{2}=(\mathsf{e}_{2},\mathsf{e}_{0}),\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle_{3}=(\mathsf{e}_{0},\mathsf{e}_{1},\mathsf{e}_{2})
.

–

Verification of
\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle
-sharing is performed as follows:

–

P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample a random
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and a bit
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{1}}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}
compute
\mathsf{x}_{1}=\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{x}_{1}
to
P_{0}
.

–

P_{1},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{y}_{1}=(\mathsf{e}_{1}+\mathsf{e}_{2})(1-2\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})+\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{r}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{0}
.

–

P_{2},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{e}_{0}(1-2\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})-\mathsf{r}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{y}_{2})
to
P_{0}
.

–

P_{0}
computes
\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{e}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}=\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\right]_{1}\oplus\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\right]_{2}\oplus\mathsf{x}_{1}
and checks if
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{x}^{\sf R}-\mathsf{y}_{1})=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{y}_{2})
.

–

If verification fails,
P_{0}
sets
\mathsf{flag}=1
, else it sets
\mathsf{flag}=0
.
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{flag}
to
P_{1}
. Next,
P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{flag}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
. Servers set
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{1}
if
\mathsf{flag}=1
.

–

If verification succeeds, servers locally convert
\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle
to
\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
by setting their shares according to Table
5
.

\justify

Online

:

–

Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}(P_{0},P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{c}^{\sf R})
where
\mathsf{c}=\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}
.

–

Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}(\mathcal{P},\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket)
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

–

Servers compute
\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket-2\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

Figure 22
:
4PC: Bit2A Protocol

While the above approach serves the purpose, we now provide an improved version, which further helps in reducing the online cost. We observe that
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
can be written as follows.
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}=(\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\beta_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}=((\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}})\oplus(\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}))^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{e}\oplus\mathsf{c})^{\sf R}=\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}-2\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}
where
\mathsf{e}=\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}
and
\mathsf{c}=\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}
. Thus, to obtain an arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
,
P_{3}
generates
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}
. To ensure the correctness of the shares, the servers
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
check whether the following equation holds:
(\mathsf{e}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}=\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-2\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
. If the verification fails, a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified. Else, this is followed by servers locally converting
\langle\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rangle
-shares to
\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
according to Table
5
, followed by multiplying
\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
and locally computing
\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket-2\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
. Note that during
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsh4}}(P_{0},P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{c}^{\sf R})
since
\alpha_{\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}}
and
\gamma_{\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}}
are set to
0
, the preprocessing of multiplication can be performed locally. The formal protocol appears in Fig.
22
.

Lemma 4.9

(Communication)
.

\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A4}}
(Fig.
22
) requires an amortized communication of
3\ell+4
bits in the preprocessing phase, and
1
round with amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

During preprocessing, one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}
requires
2\ell
bits of communication. Further, sending
\mathsf{x}_{1}
requires
1
bit, while sending
\mathsf{y}_{1}
requires
\ell
bits. Sending of
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{y}_{2})
can be amortized over several instances. Finally, communicating
\mathsf{flag}
requires
3
bits. Thus, the overall amortized communication cost in preprocessing phase is
3\ell+4
bits.
In the online phase, joint sharing of
\mathsf{c}^{\sf R}
can be performed non-interactively. The only cost is due to the online phase of multiplication which requires
3\ell
bits and one round. Thus, the amortized communication cost in the online phase is
3\ell
bits with one round of communication.
∎

Bit Injection Protocol

Given the boolean sharing of a bit
\mathsf{b}
, denoted as
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
, and the arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitinj4}}
(Fig.
23
) computes
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{b}\mathsf{v}
. This can be naively computed by servers first executing
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A4}}
on
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket^{\bf B}
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
, followed by servers computing
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
by executing
\Pi_{\mathsf{mult4}}
protocol on
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
. Instead, we provide an optimized variant which helps in reducing the communication cost of the naive approach in, both, the preprocessing and online phase. We give the details next.

Let
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}
, where
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
denotes the value of
\mathsf{b}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Then, during the computation of
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket
, we observe the following:

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}=(\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\beta_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}(\beta_{\mathsf{v}}-\alpha_{\mathsf{v}})

\displaystyle=((\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}})\oplus(\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}}))^{\sf R}((\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}})-(\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}))

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}})=(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}+(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}})\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+(2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-1)\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}

where
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}=\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}},~\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}},~\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}=\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
. The protocol proceeds with
P_{3}
generating
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}
, followed by verification of the same by
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
. If verification succeeds, then to enable
P_{2}
to compute
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{z}+\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}
,
P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}
the missing share of
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{2}
. Similarly for
P_{1}
. Next,
P_{1},P_{2}
reconstruct
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
completing the protocol.

Lemma 4.10

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitinj4}}
requires an amortized communication cost of
6\ell+4
bits in the preprocessing phase, and requires
1
round with an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

The preprocessing phase requires two instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}
which require
4\ell
bits of communication. Verifying correctness of
\langle\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rangle
requires
\ell+1
bits, whereas for
\langle\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}}\rangle
we require
\ell
bits. Finally, communicating the
\mathsf{flag}
requires
3
bits. This results in the amortized communication of
6\ell+4
bits in the preprocessing phase.
The online phase consists of three calls to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
which requires
3\ell
bits of amortized communication. Note that the last call can be deferred towards the end of the computation, thereby requiring a single round for multiple instances. Thus, the number of rounds required in the online phase is one.
∎

Let
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}=\beta_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}},~\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\gamma_{\mathsf{b}},~\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}=\beta_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}},~\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}=\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

\justify

Preprocessing

:

–

P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
sample
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
while
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample
\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

Servers execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}(P_{3},\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{ash4}}(P_{3},\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}})
. Shares of
\langle\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{v}}\rangle
are set locally as
\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{0}}=\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{2},\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{1}}=\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{v}}\right]_{1},\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{3}}=\gamma_{\mathsf{v}}
.

–

Servers verify correctness of
\langle\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\rangle
using steps similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A4}}
(Fig.
22
). Correctness of
\langle\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}}\rangle
is verified as follows.

–

P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
sample a random
\mathsf{r}_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
while
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample a random
\mathsf{r}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
set
\mathsf{a}_{0}=\mathsf{r}_{1}-\mathsf{r}_{2}
,
P_{1},P_{3}
set
\mathsf{a}_{1}=\mathsf{r}_{0}-\mathsf{r}_{1}
and
P_{2},P_{3}
set
\mathsf{a}_{2}=\mathsf{r}_{2}-\mathsf{r}_{0}
.

–

P_{1},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{x}_{1}=\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{2}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{2}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{1}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{2}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{2}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{1}}+\mathsf{a}_{1}
.

–

P_{2},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{x}_{2}=\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{0}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{0}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{0}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{2}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{2}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{0}}+\mathsf{a}_{2}
.

–

P_{0}
computes
\mathsf{x}_{0}=\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{1}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{1}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{1}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{0}}+\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{0}}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{1}}+\mathsf{a}_{0}
.

–

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{x}_{1}-\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{z}}_{1}}
to
P_{0}
, while
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{H}(-\mathsf{y}_{2})
to
P_{0}
, where
\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{x}_{2}-\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}_{2}}
.

–

P_{0}
computes
\mathsf{y}_{0}=\mathsf{x}_{0}-\mathsf{e}_{\mathsf{z}_{0}}
, and checks if
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{y}_{0}+\mathsf{y}_{1})=\mathsf{H}(-\mathsf{y}_{2})
.

–

If verification fails,
P_{0}
sets
\mathsf{flag}=1
, else it sets
\mathsf{flag}=0
.
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{flag}
to
P_{1}
. Next,
P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{flag}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
. Servers set
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{1}
if
\mathsf{flag}=1
.

\justify

Online

:

–

P_{0},P_{1}
compute
\mathsf{u}_{1}=-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{1}}+(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}})\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{1}}^{\sf R}+(2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-1)\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{z}}_{1}}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{1}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{u}_{1}
to
P_{2}
.

–

P_{0},P_{2}
compute
\mathsf{u}_{2}=-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{0}}+(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}})\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{0}}^{\sf R}+(2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-1)\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{z}}_{0}}+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{2}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\mathsf{u}_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}
compute
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{u}_{1}+\mathsf{u}_{2}-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{v}}_{2}}+(\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}-2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}})\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{b}}_{2}}^{\sf R}+(2\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}-1)\mathsf{e}_{{\mathsf{z}}_{2}}+\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
.

Figure 23
:
4PC: Bit Injection Protocol

Dot Product

Given
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of two
\mathsf{n}
-sized vectors
\vec{\mathbf{x}},\vec{\mathbf{y}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp4}}
(Fig.
24
) enables servers to compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket
with
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
. The protocol is essentially similar to
\mathsf{n}
instances of multiplications of the form
\mathsf{z}_{i}=\mathsf{x}_{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}
for
i\in[\mathsf{n}]
. But instead of communicating values corresponding to each of the
\mathsf{n}
instances, servers locally sum up the shares and communicate a single value. This helps to obtain a communication cost independent of the size of the vectors.

In more detail, the dot product protocol proceeds as follows.
During the preprocessing phase, similar to the multiplication protocol
P_{0},P_{1},P_{3}
sample a random
\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{1}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{2}=\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}-\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
.

P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
sample a random
\psi
, and generate its
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares locally. Servers
P_{3},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
then compute
\left[\chi\right]_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{j}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{j})+\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{j}-\left[\psi\right]_{j}
, and
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{j}
to
P_{0}
.
The formal protocol is given in Fig.
24
.

\justify

Preprocessing

:

–

P_{0},P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, sample random
\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, while
P_{0},P_{1},P_{3}
sample random
\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

P_{1}
,
P_{2}
,
P_{3}
together sample random
\gamma_{\mathsf{z}},\psi,\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and set
\left[\psi\right]_{1}=\mathsf{r},~\left[\psi\right]_{2}=\psi-\mathsf{r}
.

–

P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{2}=\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}-\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{1}
, where
\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}
.

P_{0},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
.

–

P_{3},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, set
\left[\chi\right]_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{j}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{j})\allowbreak+\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{j}-\left[\psi\right]_{j}
.

–

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{1}
to
P_{0}
, and
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{2}
to
P_{0}
.

\justify

Online

:

–

P_{0},P_{j}
, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, compute
\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{j}=-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}((\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{x}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}\right]_{j}+(\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\right]_{j})+\left[\alpha_{\mathsf{z}}\right]_{j}+\left[\chi\right]_{j}
.

–

P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2},P_{0}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
computes
\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}=\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1}+\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
and sets
\beta_{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{n}}(\beta_{\mathsf{x}_{i}}\beta_{\mathsf{y}_{i}})+\psi
.

–

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
.

Figure 24
:
4PC: Dot Product Protocol (
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}
)

Lemma 4.11

(Communication)
.

\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp4}}
(Fig.
24
) requires an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the preprocessing phase, and
1
round and an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits in the online phase.

Proof.

The preprocessing phase requires three calls to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
, one to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\Gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{x}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{y}}}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2}
, and two to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\chi\right]_{1},\left[\chi\right]_{2}
to
P_{0}
. Each invocation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
requires
\ell
bits resulting in the amortized communication cost of preprocessing phase to be
3\ell
bits.
In the online phase, there are
2
parallel invocations of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{1},\left[\mathsf{\beta}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\star}\right]_{2}
to
P_{2},P_{1}
, respectively, which require amortized communication of
2\ell
bits and one round. This is followed by another call to
\Pi_{\mathsf{jmp4}}
to
\mathsf{jmp4\mbox{-}send}

\beta_{\mathsf{z}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{z}}
to
P_{0}
which requires one more round and amortized communication of
\ell
bits. As in the multiplication protocol, this step can be delayed till the end of the protocol and clubbed for multiple instances. Thus, the online phase requires one round and an amortized communication of
